_I_n_t_e_r_f_a_c_e _t_o _b_o_w_t_i_e

_D_e_s_c_r_i_p_t_i_o_n:

     The following functions can be used to call the bowtie and
     bowtie-build binaries.

     We recommend to use the ‘QuasR’ package instead of using ‘bowtie’
     and ‘bowtie_build’ directly. ‘QuasR’ provides a simpler interface
     than ‘Rbowtie’ and covers the whole analysis workflow of typical
     ultra-high throughput sequencing experiments, starting from the
     raw sequence reads, over pre-processing and alignment, up to
     quantification.

_U_s_a_g_e:

     bowtie_build(references, outdir, ..., prefix = "index", force = FALSE,
     strict = TRUE, execute = TRUE)

     outfile, force = FALSE, strict = TRUE, execute = TRUE)

...skipping 23 lines

     ...: Additional arguments to be passed on to the binaries. See
          below for details.

_D_e_t_a_i_l_s:

     All additional arguments in ‘...’ are interpreted as additional
     parameters to be passed on to the binaries. For flags, those are
     supposed to be logicals (e.g., ‘quiet=TRUE’ will be translated
     into ‘--q’, ‘q=TRUE’ in ‘-q’, and so on). Parameters with
     additional input are supposed to be character or numeric vectors,
     where the individual vector elements are collapsed into a single
     comma-separated string (e.g., ‘k=2’ is translated into ‘k 2’,
     ‘bmax=100’ into ‘--bmax 100’, ‘3=letters[1:3]’ into ‘-3 a,b,c’,
     and so on). Note that some arguments to the bowtie binary will be
     ignored if they are already handled as explicit function
     arguments. See the output of ‘bowtie_usage()’ and
     ‘bowtie_build_usage()’ for details about available parameters.

_V_a_l_u_e:

     The output generated by calling the binaries. For ‘bowtie_build’
     this is typically a report of the index generation, for ‘bowtie’
--More--
     this can be a vector of aligments (if ‘outfile’ is missing),
     otherwise an empty character scalar.

     ‘bowtie_usage()’ and ‘bowtie_build_usage()’ return the usage
     information for the respective binaries.

     ‘bowtie_version()’ return the bowtie versions information.

     Florian Hahne

     td <- tempdir()
     
     ## Building a bowtie index
     refs <- dir(system.file(package="Rbowtie", "samples", "refs"),
     full=TRUE)
     tmp <- bowtie_build(references=refs, outdir=file.path(td, "index"),
     force=TRUE)
     head(tmp)
     dir(file.path(td, "index"))
     tmp2 <- bowtie_build(references=refs, outdir=file.path(td,"indexColor"),
     force=TRUE, C=TRUE)
     dir(file.path(td, "indexColor"))
     head(tmp2)
     
     
     ## Alignments
     reads <- system.file(package="Rbowtie", "samples", "reads", "reads.fastq")
     tmp <- bowtie(sequences=reads, index=file.path(td, "index", "index"))
     tmp
     bowtie(sequences=reads, index=file.path(td, "index", "index"),
     outfile=file.path(td, "alignments.txt"), best=TRUE, force=TRUE)
     readLines(file.path(td, "alignments.txt"))

stderr_text.splitlines():\n        if line.startswith('# reads processed:'):\n            stats['reads_processed'] = int(line.split(':',1)[1].strip())\n        elif line.startswith('# reads with at least one alignment:'):\n            m = re.search(r':\\s*([0-9,]+)', line)\n            if m: stats['reads_with_at_least_one_alignment'] = int(m.group(1).replace(',',''))\n        elif line.startswith('# reads that failed to align:'):\n            m = re.search(r':\\s*([0-9,]+)', line)\n            if m: stats['reads_failed_to_align'] = int(m.group(1).replace(',',''))\n        elif line.startswith('# reads with alignments suppressed due to -m:'):\n            m = re.search(r':\\s*([0-9,]+)', line)\n            if m: stats['alignments_suppressed_due_to_m'] = int(m.group(1).replace(',',''))\n        elif line.startswith('Reported '):\n            m = re.search(r'Reported\\s+([0-9,]+)\\s+alignments', line)\n            if m: stats['reported_alignments'] = int(m.group(1).replace(',',''))\n    return stats\n\n\ndef worker(arg):\n    idx, lib, run_acc = arg\n    fq = f\"/data/raw/GSE154899/fastq/{run_acc}.fastq.gz\"\n    err = f\"/workspace/work/{lib}.bowtie.err\"\n    awk_script = 'NF>=3 {count[$3]++} END {for (k in count) print k \"\\\\t\" count[k]}'\n    inner = f\"pigz -dc {shlex.quote(fq)} | /usr/local/lib/R/site-library/Rbowtie/bowtie -q -n 2 -l 28 -m 1 --best --strata -p 2 -x {index_prefix} - 2>{shlex.quote(err)} | awk -F '\\\\t' '{awk_script}'\"\n    proc = subprocess.Popen(['bash','-lc',inner], stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.PIPE, text=True)\n    arr = np.zeros(n_probes, dtype=np.int32)\n    for line in proc.stdout:\n        line = line.rstrip('\\n')\n        if not line:\n            continue\n        k, v = line.split('\\t')\n        arr[NAME_TO_IDX[k]] = int(v)\n    stderr_text = proc.stderr.read()\n    ret = proc.wait()\n    err_text = Path(err).read_text() if Path(err).exists() else stderr_text\n    stats = parse_bowtie_stats(err_text)\n    return idx, lib, arr, ret, stats\n\nremaining = [(i, row['library_id'], row['run_accession']) for i, row in m.iterrows() if row['library_id'] not in completed]\nprint('remaining before batch', len(remaining))\nthis_batch = remaining[:BATCH]\nprint('processing', [x[1] for x in this_batch])\nstart = time.perf_counter()\nwith concurrent.futures.ProcessPoolExecutor(max_workers=3) as ex:\n    futs = [ex.submit(worker, a) for a in this_batch]\n    for fut in concurrent.futures.as_completed(futs):\n        idx, lib, arr, ret, stats = fut.result()\n        if ret != 0:\n            raise RuntimeError(f'Bowtie command failed for {lib} with exit code {ret}')\n        counts[:, idx] = arr\n        counts.flush()\n        rec = {'library_id': lib, 'column_index': int(idx), **stats}\n        completed[lib] = rec\n        qc_rows.append(rec)\n        tmp = progress_json.with_suffix('.json.tmp')\n        tmp.write_text(json.dumps({'completed': qc_rows}, indent=2))\n        tmp.replace(progress_json)\n        print('done', lib, 'sum', int(arr.sum()), 'reported', stats['reported_alignments'])\n\nelapsed = time.perf_counter() - start\nprint(f'batch elapsed {elapsed/60:.1f} min')\nprint('completed total', len(completed))\nif len(completed) == n_samples:\n    qc_df = pd.DataFrame(qc_rows).sort_values('column_index')\n    qc_df.to_csv(qc_tsv, sep='\\t', index=False)\n    count_df = pd.DataFrame(np.asarray(counts), index=probe_names, columns=m['library_id'].tolist())\n    count_df.index.name = 'gene_probe_id'\n    count_csv = outputs / 'tempo_seq_filtered_counts.csv'\n    count_df.to_csv(count_csv)\n    print('Wrote final outputs')\n"
}

a in this_batch]\n    for fut in concurrent.futures.as_completed(futs):\n        idx, lib, arr, ret, stats = fut.result()\n        if ret != 0:\n            raise RuntimeError(f'Bowtie command failed qc_rows.append(rec)\n        tmp = progress_json.with_suffix('.json.tmp')\n        tmp.write_text(json.dumps({'completed': qc_rows}, indent=2))\n        tmp.replace(progress_json)\n        print('done', lib, 'sum', int(arr.sum()), 'reported', stats['reported_alignments'])\n\nelapsed = time.perf_counter() - start\nprint(f'batch elapsed {elapsed/60:.1f} min')\nprint('completed total', _G_e_n_e _s_e_t _a_n_a_l_y_s_i_s

_D_e_s_c_r_i_p_t_i_o_n:

     Performs gene set analysis (GSA) based on a given number of
     gene-level statistics and a gene set collection, using a variety
     of available methods, returning the gene set statistics and
     p-values of different directionality classes.

       signifMethod = "geneSampling",

       gsc,
       gsSizeLim = c(1, Inf),
       permStats = NULL,
       permDirections = NULL,

_A_r_g_u_m_e_n_t_s:

geneLevelStats: a vector or a one-column data.frame or matrix,
          containing the gene level statistics. Gene level statistics
          can be e.g. p-values, t-values or F-values.

directions: a vector or a one-column data.frame or matrix, containing
          fold-change like values for the related gene-level
          statistics. This is mainly used if statistics are p-values or
          F-values, but not required. The values should be positive or
          negative, but only the sign information will be used, so the
          actual value will not matter.

geneSetStat: the statistical GSA method to use. Can be one of
          ‘"fisher"’, ‘"stouffer"’, ‘"reporter"’, ‘"tailStrength"’,
          ‘"wilcoxon"’, ‘"mean"’, ‘"median"’, ‘"sum"’, ‘"maxmean"’,
          ‘"gsea"’, ‘"fgsea"’ or ‘"page"’. See below for details.
--More--
        
--More--
        
No previous regular expression

                              
signifMethod: the method for significance assessment of gene sets, i.e.
          p-value calculation. Can be one of ‘"geneSampling"’,
          ‘"samplePermutation"’ or ‘"nullDist"’

adjMethod: the method for adjusting for multiple testing. Can be any of
          the methods supported by ‘p.adjust’, i.e. ‘"holm"’,
          ‘"hochberg"’, ‘"hommel"’, ‘"bonferroni"’, ‘"BH"’, ‘"BY"’,
          ‘"fdr"’ or ‘"none"’. The exception is for
          ‘geneSetStat="gsea"’, where only the options ‘"fdr"’ and
          ‘"none"’ can be used.

     gsc: a gene set collection given as an object of class ‘GSC’ as
          returned by the ‘loadGSC’ function.

gsSizeLim: a vector of length two, giving the minimum and maximum gene
          set size (number of member genes) to be kept for the
          analysis. Defaults to ‘c(1,Inf)’.

permStats: a matrix with permutated gene-level statistics (columns) for
          each gene (rows). This should be calculated by the user by
          randomizing the sample labels in the original data, and
          recalculating the gene level statistics for each comparison a

          large number of times, thus generating a vector (rows in the
          matrix) of background statistics for each gene. This argument
          is required and only used if
          ‘signifMethod="samplePermutation"’.

permDirections: similar to ‘permStats’, but should instead contain
          fold-change like values for the related permutated
          statistics. This is mainly used if the statistics are
          p-values or F-values, but not required. The values should be
          positive or negative, but only the sign information will be
          used, so the actual value will not matter. This argument is
          only used if ‘signifMethod="samplePermutation"’, but not
          required. Note however, that if ‘directions’ is give then
          also ‘permDirections’ is required, and vice versa.

   nPerm: the number of permutations to use for gene sampling, i.e. if
          ‘signifMethod="geneSampling"’. The original Reporter features
          algorithm (‘geneSetStat="reporter"’ and
          ‘signifMethod="nullDist"’) also uses a permutation step which
          is controlled by ‘nPerm’.

gseaParam: the exponent parameter of the GSEA and FGSEA approach. This
          defaults to 1, as recommended by the GSEA authors.
--More--

        
   ncpus: the number of cpus to use. If larger than 1, the gene
          permutation part will be run in parallel and thus decrease
          runtime. Requires R package ‘snowfall’ to be installed.
          Should be set so that ‘nPerm/ncpus’ is a positive integer.
          (Not used by FGSEA.)

 verbose: a logical. Whether or not to display progress messages during
          the analysis.

     ‘gsc’. If ‘geneSetStat’ is set to ‘"fisher"’, ‘"stouffer"’,
     ‘"reporter"’ or ‘"tailStrength"’ only p-values are allowed as

...skipping 1 line

     ‘signifMethod="geneSampling"’ or
     ‘signifMethod="samplePermutation"’ can be used, except for
     ‘"fgsea"’ where only ‘signifMethod="geneSampling"’ is allowed. If
     ‘signifMethod="geneSampling"’ gene sampling is used, meaning that
     the gene labels are randomized ‘nPerm’ times and the gene set
     statistics are recalculated so that a background distribution for
     each original gene set is acquired. The gene set p-values are
     calculated based on this background distribution. Similarly if
     ‘signifMethod="samplePermutation"’ sample permutation is used. In
     this case the argument ‘permStats’ (and optionally
     ‘permDirections’) has to be supplied.

     The ‘runGSA’ function returns p-values for each gene set.
     Depending on the choice of methods and gene statistics up to three
     classes of p-values can be calculated, describing different
     aspects of regulation directionality. The three directionality
     classes are Distinct-directional, Mixed-directional and
     Non-directional. The non-directional p-values (‘pNonDirectional’)
     are calculated based on absolute values of the gene statistics (or
     p-values without sign information), meaning that gene sets
     containing a high portion of significant genes, independent of
     direction, will turn up significant. That is, gene-sets with a low
     ‘pNonDirectional’ should be interpreted to be significantly
--More--
     affected by gene regulation, but there can be a mix of both up and
     down regulation involved. The mixed-directional p-values
     (‘pMixedDirUp’ and ‘pMixedDirDn’) are calculated using the subset
     of the gene statistics that are up-regulated and down-regulated,
     respectively. This means that a gene set with a low ‘pMixedDirUp’
     will have a component of significantly up-regulated genes,
     disregardful of the extent of down-regulated genes, and the
     reverse for ‘pMixedDirDn’. This also means that one can get gene
     sets that are both significantly affected by down-regulation and
     significantly affected by up-regulation at the same time. Note
     that sample permutation cannot be used to calculate ‘pMixedDirUp’
     and ‘pMixedDirDn’ since the subset sizes will differ. Finally, the
     distinct-directional p-values (‘pDistinctDirup’ and
     ‘pDistinctDirDn’) are calculated from statistics with sign
     information (e.g. t-statistics). In this case, if a gene set
     contains both up- and down-regulated genes, they will cancel out
     each other. A gene-set with a low ‘pDistinctDirUp’ will be
     significantly affected by up-regulation, but not a mix of up- and
     down-regulation (as in the case of the mixed-directional and
     non-directional p-values). In order to be able to calculate
     distinct-directional gene set p-values while using p-values as
     gene-level statistics, the gene-level p-values are transformed as
     follows: The up-regulated portion of the p-values are divided by 2
--More--
        
162
   
No previous regular expression
     (scaled to range between 0-0.5) and the down-regulated portion of
     p-values are set to 1-p/2 (scaled to range between 1-0.5). This
     means that a significantly down-regulated gene will get a p-value
     close to 1. These new p-values are used as input to the gene-set
     analysis procedure to get ‘pDistinctDirUp’. Similarly, the
     opposite is done, so that the up-regulated portion is scaled
     between 1-0.5 and the down-regulated between 0-0.5 to get the
     ‘pDistinctDirDn’.

geneStatType: The interpretated type of gene-level statistics

geneSetStat: The method for gene set statistic calculation

signifMethod: The method for significance estimation

adjMethod: The method of adjustment for multiple testing

    info: A list object with detailed info number of genes and gene

orthologs              package:babelgene               R Documentation

_R_e_t_r_i_e_v_e _g_e_n_e _o_r_t_h_o_l_o_g_s/_h_o_m_o_l_o_g_s

_D_e_s_c_r_i_p_t_i_o_n:

     Retrieve gene orthologs/homologs for a set of genes. Converts
     between human and non-human analogs.

_U_s_a_g_e:

     orthologs(genes, species, human = TRUE, min_support = 3, top = TRUE)
     
_A_r_g_u_m_e_n_t_s:

   genes: A vector of gene symbols or Entrez/Ensembl IDs.

 species: Species name, such as ‘Mus musculus’ or ‘mouse’ (see
          ‘species()’ for options).

   human: A logical scalar indicating if the input genes are human. If
          ‘TRUE’, the input genes are human. If ‘FALSE’, the input
          genes correspond to the non-human species and the output will
          be the human equivalents.

min_support: Minimum number of supporting source databases. Gene pairs
          available in this package are supported by 2 to 12 databases
          (the maximum varies depending on the species).

     top: For each gene, output only the match with the highest support
          level if there are multiple hits.

_V_a_l_u_e:

     A data frame of gene pairs (human and given species).

_R_e_f_e_r_e_n_c_e_s:

     Wright MW, Eyre TA, Lush MJ, Povey S, Bruford EA. HCOP: the HGNC
     comparison of orthology predictions search tool. _Mamm Genome_.
     2005 Nov;16(11):827-8. doi:10.1007/s00335-005-0103-2
     <https://doi.org/10.1007/s00335-005-0103-2>

     Eyre TA, Wright MW, Lush MJ, Bruford EA. HCOP: a searchable
     database of human orthology predictions. _Brief Bioinform_. 2007
     Jan;8(1):2-5. doi:10.1093/bib/bbl030
     <https://doi.org/10.1093/bib/bbl030>

#!/usr/bin/env Rscript
# TempO-Seq liver transcriptome analysis pipeline.
# Assumptions/substitutions: Bowtie counting used the QuasR/Rbowtie default seed-mode settings (-n 2 -l 28 -m 1 --best --strata) because they match the QuasR vignette defaults and are equivalent to the staged TempO-Seq counting convention. Ensembl archive access for biomaRt was blocked by the proxy, so mouse-to-human ortholog mapping was performed with babelgene::orthologs (mouse->human, human=FALSE, top=TRUE, min_support=3) as an offline substitute. For gene-set analysis, probes were collapsed to one mouse gene (largest absolute Wald statistic) and then to one human ortholog (largest absolute Wald statistic among mouse genes mapping to the same human symbol).

suppressPackageStartupMessages({
  library(data.table)
  library(DESeq2)
  library(piano)
  library(babelgene)
})

set.seed(1)
options(stringsAsFactors = FALSE)

#-----------------------------
# Paths
#-----------------------------
counts_path <- "outputs/tempo_seq_filtered_counts.csv"
sample_path <- "data/metadata/sample_metadata.tsv"
manifest_path <- "data/metadata/temposeq/100770_mus_musculus_wt_11_full_manifest_revB.csv"
c2_path <- "work/c2cp_v6.2_gene_sets.tsv"
hallmark_path <- "work/hallmark_v6.2_gene_sets.tsv"
dna_path <- "work/dna_damage_gene_sets_raw.tsv"

out_de <- "outputs/deseq2_significant_degs.csv"
out_piano <- "outputs/piano_preranked_gsea.csv"
out_ora <- "outputs/hypergeometric_pathway_enrichment.csv"
out_dna <- "outputs/dna_damage_enrichment.csv"

# Intermediate files
map_out <- "work/mouse_to_human_ortholog_map.tsv"
canon_sets_out <- "work/canonical_gene_sets_human_filtered.tsv"
dna_sets_out <- "work/dna_damage_gene_sets_human_filtered.tsv"
canon_bg_out <- "work/canonical_background_genes.txt"
dna_bg_out <- "work/dna_damage_background_genes.txt"

canon_bg_out <- "work/canonical_background_genes.txt"

#-----------------------------
# Helpers
#-----------------------------
split_genes <- function(x) {
  x <- as.character(x)
  x <- x[!is.na(x) & nzchar(x)]
  unique(trimws(unlist(strsplit(x, ";", fixed = TRUE), use.names = FALSE)))
}

collapse_to_human <- function(res_dt, probe_map, orth_map) {
  dt <- merge(res_dt, probe_map, by = "gene_probe_id", all.x = TRUE, sort = FALSE)
  dt <- dt[!is.na(mouse_symbol) & nzchar(mouse_symbol)]
  setorder(dt, -abs(stat))
  dt <- dt[!duplicated(mouse_symbol)]
  dt <- merge(dt, orth_map[, .(mouse_symbol, human_symbol)], by = "mouse_symbol", all.x = TRUE, sort = FALSE)
  dt <- dt[!is.na(human_symbol) & nzchar(human_symbol)]
  setorder(dt, -abs(stat))
  dt <- dt[!duplicated(human_symbol)]
  dt
}

make_human_vector <- function(res_dt, probe_map, orth_map) {
  dt <- collapse_to_human(res_dt, probe_map, orth_map)
  vec <- dt$stat
  names(vec) <- dt$human_symbol
  vec <- vec[!is.na(vec)]
  vec <- sort(vec, decreasing = TRUE)
  vec
}

filter_gene_sets <- function(gs_list, background) {
  gs_list <- lapply(gs_list, function(gs) intersect(unique(gs), background))
  gs_list <- gs_list[lengths(gs_list) >= 5 & lengths(gs_list) <= 500]
  gs_list
}

hypergeom_table <- function(sig_genes, background, gene_sets) {
  sig_genes <- unique(sig_genes)
  background <- unique(background)
  gene_sets <- lapply(gene_sets, function(gs) intersect(unique(gs), background))
  gene_sets <- gene_sets[lengths(gene_sets) > 0]
  bg_n <- length(background)

#!/usr/bin/env Rscript
# TempO-Seq liver transcriptome analysis pipeline.
# Assumptions/substitutions: Bowtie counting used the QuasR/Rbowtie default seed-mode settings (-n 2 -l 28 -m 1 --best --strata) because they match the QuasR vignette defaults and are equivalent to the staged TempO-Seq counting convention. Ensembl archive access for biomaRt was blocked by the proxy, so mouse-to-human ortholog mapping was performed with babelgene::orthologs (mouse->human, human=FALSE, top=TRUE, min_support=3) as an offline substitute. For gene-set analysis, probes were collapsed to one mouse gene (largest absolute Wald statistic) and then to one human ortholog (largest absolute Wald statistic among mouse genes mapping to the same human symbol).

suppressPackageStartupMessages({
  library(data.table)
  library(DESeq2)
  library(piano)
  library(babelgene)
})

set.seed(1)
options(stringsAsFactors = FALSE)

#-----------------------------
# Paths
#-----------------------------
counts_path <- "outputs/tempo_seq_filtered_counts.csv"
sample_path <- "data/metadata/sample_metadata.tsv"
manifest_path <- "data/metadata/temposeq/100770_mus_musculus_wt_11_full_manifest_revB.csv"
c2_path <- "work/c2cp_v6.2_gene_sets.tsv"
hallmark_path <- "work/hallmark_v6.2_gene_sets.tsv"
dna_path <- "work/dna_damage_gene_sets_raw.tsv"

out_de <- "outputs/deseq2_significant_degs.csv"
out_piano <- "outputs/piano_preranked_gsea.csv"
out_ora <- "outputs/hypergeometric_pathway_enrichment.csv"
out_dna <- "outputs/dna_damage_enrichment.csv"

# Intermediate files
map_out <- "work/mouse_to_human_ortholog_map.tsv"
canon_sets_out <- "work/canonical_gene_sets_human_filtered.tsv"
dna_sets_out <- "work/dna_damage_gene_sets_human_filtered.tsv"
canon_bg_out <- "work/canonical_background_genes.txt"
canon_gmt_out <- "work/canonical_gene_sets_human_filtered.gmt"
dna_bg_out <- "work/dna_damage_background_genes.txt"

#-----------------------------
# Helpers
#-----------------------------
split_genes <- function(x) {
  x <- as.character(x)
  x <- x[!is.na(x) & nzchar(x)]
  unique(trimws(unlist(strsplit(x, ";", fixed = TRUE), use.names = FALSE)))
}

collapse_to_human <- function(res_dt, probe_map, orth_map) {
  dt <- merge(res_dt, probe_map, by = "gene_probe_id", all.x = TRUE, sort = FALSE)
  dt <- dt[!is.na(mouse_symbol) & nzchar(mouse_symbol)]
  setorder(dt, -abs(stat))
  dt <- dt[!duplicated(mouse_symbol)]
  dt <- merge(dt, orth_map[, .(mouse_symbol, human_symbol)], by = "mouse_symbol", all.x = TRUE, sort = FALSE)
  dt <- dt[!is.na(human_symbol) & nzchar(human_symbol)]
  setorder(dt, -abs(stat))
  dt <- dt[!duplicated(human_symbol)]
  dt
}

make_human_vector <- function(res_dt, probe_map, orth_map) {
  dt <- collapse_to_human(res_dt, probe_map, orth_map)
  vec <- dt$stat
  names(vec) <- dt$human_symbol
  vec <- vec[!is.na(vec)]
  vec <- sort(vec, decreasing = TRUE)
  vec
}

filter_gene_sets <- function(gs_list, background) {
  gs_list <- lapply(gs_list, function(gs) intersect(unique(gs), background))
  gs_list <- gs_list[lengths(gs_list) >= 5 & lengths(gs_list) <= 500]
  gs_list
}

hypergeom_table <- function(sig_genes, background, gene_sets) {
  sig_genes <- unique(sig_genes)
  background <- unique(background)
  gene_sets <- lapply(gene_sets, function(gs) intersect(unique(gs), background))
  gene_sets <- gene_sets[lengths(gene_sets) > 0]
  bg_n <- length(background)
  sig_n <- length(sig_genes)
  if (sig_n == 0 || bg_n == 0 || length(gene_sets) == 0) {
    out <- data.table(gene_set = names(gene_sets), p_value = 1, significant_in_gene_set = 0)
    return(out)
  }
  gene_set_names <- names(gene_sets)
  set_sizes <- lengths(gene_sets)
  overlaps <- vapply(gene_sets, function(gs) length(intersect(sig_genes, gs)), integer(1))
  pvals <- mapply(function(k, m) {
    if (k <= 0) return(1)
    phyper(k - 1, m, bg_n - m, sig_n, lower.tail = FALSE)
  }, overlaps, set_sizes, SIMPLIFY = TRUE, USE.NAMES = FALSE)
  data.table(gene_set = gene_set_names, p_value = as.numeric(pvals), significant_in_gene_set = as.integer(overlaps))
}

run_piano_contrast <- function(rank_vec, gsc, timepoint, concentration) {
  gsa <- runGSA(
    geneLevelStats = rank_vec,
    gsc = gsc,
    geneSetStat = "fgsea",
    nPerm = 1000,
    gsSizeLim = c(5, 500),
    verbose = FALSE
  )
  tab <- as.data.table(GSAsummaryTable(gsa))
  if (!nrow(tab)) return(data.table())
  setnames(tab, old = c("Name", "Genes (tot)", "Stat (dist.dir)", "p (dist.dir.up)", "p adj (dist.dir.up)", "p (dist.dir.dn)", "p adj (dist.dir.dn)", "Genes (up)", "Genes (down)"),
                new = c("gene_set", "genes_total", "stat_dist_dir", "p_up", "fdr_up", "p_down", "fdr_down", "genes_up", "genes_down"), skip_absent = TRUE)
  tab[, direction := ifelse(stat_dist_dir > 0, "up", "down")]
  tab[, p_value := ifelse(direction == "up", p_up, p_down)]
  tab[, fdr := ifelse(direction == "up", fdr_up, fdr_down)]
  tab <- tab[!is.na(p_value) & !is.na(fdr) & fdr < 0.10]
  if (!nrow(tab)) return(data.table())
  tab[, percent_up_genes := 100 * genes_up / genes_total]
  tab[, percent_down_genes := 100 * genes_down / genes_total]
  tab[, `:=`(timepoint = timepoint, concentration = concentration)]
  tab[, gsea_result_id := paste(timepoint, concentration, direction, gene_set, sep = "|")]
  tab <- tab[, .(gsea_result_id, timepoint, concentration, direction, gene_set, genes_total, stat_dist_dir, p_value, fdr, genes_up, genes_down, percent_up_genes, percent_down_genes)]
  setorder(tab, timepoint, concentration, direction, fdr, gene_set)
  tab
}

run_ora_contrast <- function(sig_all, sig_up, sig_down, background, gene_sets, timepoint, concentration, keep_all = FALSE) {
  directions <- list(non_directional = sig_all, up = sig_up, down = sig_down)
  out <- list()
  for (d in names(directions)) {
    tt <- hypergeom_table(directions[[d]], background, gene_sets)
    if (!nrow(tt)) next
    tt[, fdr := p.adjust(p_value, method = "BH")]
    tt[, `:=`(timepoint = timepoint, concentration = concentration, direction = d)]
    tt[, hypergeometric_result_id := paste(timepoint, concentration, direction, gene_set, sep = "|")]
    tt <- tt[, .(hypergeometric_result_id, timepoint, concentration, direction, gene_set, p_value, fdr, significant_in_gene_set)]
2026-11-15T03:30:56.441959+00:00 · bash_session · { "action": "type_submit", "input": "sed -n '220,380p' /workspace/work/analysis_pipeline.R" }
{
  "action": "type_submit",
  "input": "sed -n '220,380p' /workspace/work/analysis_pipeline.R"
}

    if (!keep_all) tt <- tt[fdr < 0.10]
    if (nrow(tt)) out[[d]] <- tt
  }
  if (!length(out)) return(data.table())
  ans <- rbindlist(out, use.names = TRUE, fill = TRUE)
  setorder(ans, timepoint, concentration, direction, fdr, gene_set)
  ans
}

run_dna_ora <- function(sig_all, sig_up, sig_down, background, gene_sets, timepoint, concentration) {
  tt_non <- hypergeom_table(sig_all, background, gene_sets)
  setnames(tt_non, c("p_value", "significant_in_gene_set"), c("p_value_non_directional", "significant_in_gene_set_non_directional"))
  tt_non[, fdr_non_directional := p.adjust(p_value_non_directional, method = "BH")]

  tt_up <- hypergeom_table(sig_up, background, gene_sets)
  setnames(tt_up, c("p_value", "significant_in_gene_set"), c("p_value_up", "significant_in_gene_set_up"))
  tt_up[, fdr_up := p.adjust(p_value_up, method = "BH")]

  tt_down <- hypergeom_table(sig_down, background, gene_sets)
  setnames(tt_down, c("p_value", "significant_in_gene_set"), c("p_value_down", "significant_in_gene_set_down"))
  tt_down[, fdr_down := p.adjust(p_value_down, method = "BH")]

  out <- Reduce(function(x, y) merge(x, y, by = "gene_set", all = TRUE, sort = FALSE), list(tt_non, tt_up, tt_down))
  out[, `:=`(timepoint = timepoint, concentration = concentration)]
  out[, dna_damage_result_id := paste(timepoint, concentration, gene_set, sep = "|")]
  out <- out[, .(dna_damage_result_id, timepoint, concentration, gene_set,
                 p_value_non_directional, fdr_non_directional, significant_in_gene_set_non_directional,
                 p_value_up, fdr_up, significant_in_gene_set_up,
                 p_value_down, fdr_down, significant_in_gene_set_down)]
  setorder(out, timepoint, concentration, gene_set)
  out
}

#-----------------------------
# Inputs

samples <- fread(sample_path)
manifest <- fread(manifest_path)

# verify and prepare count matrix

stopifnot(all(colnames(counts_dt)[-1] == samples$library_id))
count_mat <- as.matrix(counts_dt[, -1, with = FALSE])
rownames(count_mat) <- counts_dt$gene_probe_id
storage.mode(count_mat) <- "integer"

samples[, timepoint := as.character(timepoint)]
samples[, treatment_ppm := as.character(treatment_ppm)]
time_levels <- c("day7", "day28", "day90")
conc_levels <- c("0ppm", "40ppm", "200ppm", "600ppm", "2000ppm", "6000ppm")
samples[, timepoint := factor(timepoint, levels = time_levels)]
samples[, treatment_ppm := factor(treatment_ppm, levels = conc_levels)]

# probe -> mouse gene symbol mapping
probe_map <- manifest[, .(gene_probe_id = PROBE_NAME, mouse_symbol = GENE_SYMBOL)]
probe_map <- probe_map[!is.na(mouse_symbol) & nzchar(mouse_symbol)]

# Mouse-to-human ortholog map (offline substitute for blocked Ensembl archive)
mouse_genes <- unique(probe_map$mouse_symbol)
orth <- orthologs(mouse_genes, species = "mouse", human = FALSE, min_support = 3, top = TRUE)
orth <- as.data.table(orth)
if (!all(c("symbol", "human_symbol") %in% names(orth))) stop("Unexpected babelgene ortholog table structure")
setnames(orth, old = c("symbol", "human_symbol"), new = c("mouse_symbol", "human_symbol"))
orth <- orth[!is.na(mouse_symbol) & nzchar(mouse_symbol) & !is.na(human_symbol) & nzchar(human_symbol)]
orth <- unique(orth[, .(mouse_symbol, human_symbol, support_n)])
fwrite(orth, map_out, sep = "\t")

human_background <- sort(unique(orth$human_symbol))
writeLines(human_background, canon_bg_out)

# canonical pathway gene sets (human symbols already)
canon_sets <- lapply(canon_raw$genes, split_genes)
names(canon_sets) <- canon_raw$gene_set
canon_sets <- filter_gene_sets(canon_sets, human_background)
canon_sets_dt <- rbindlist(lapply(names(canon_sets), function(nm) data.table(gene_set = nm, genes = paste(canon_sets[[nm]], collapse = ";"))), use.names = TRUE)
fwrite(canon_sets_dt, canon_sets_out, sep = "\t")
canon_gmt_lines <- vapply(names(canon_sets), function(nm) paste(c(nm, "na", canon_sets[[nm]]), collapse = "\t"), character(1))
writeLines(canon_gmt_lines, canon_gmt_out)
canon_gsc <- loadGSC(canon_gmt_out)

# curated DNA-damage gene sets (human symbols)

dna_sets <- lapply(dna_raw$genes, split_genes)
names(dna_sets) <- dna_raw$gene_set
dna_sets <- filter_gene_sets(dna_sets, unique(unlist(dna_sets, use.names = FALSE)))

fwrite(dna_sets_dt, dna_sets_out, sep = "\t")

#-----------------------------
# DESeq2 per timepoint
#-----------------------------
deseq_out_list <- list()
contrast_results <- list()
for (tp in time_levels) {
  smp <- samples[timepoint == tp]
  cmat <- count_mat[, smp$library_id, drop = FALSE]
  coldata <- data.frame(row.names = smp$library_id, treatment_ppm = factor(as.character(smp$treatment_ppm), levels = conc_levels))
  dds <- DESeqDataSetFromMatrix(countData = round(cmat), colData = coldata, design = ~ treatment_ppm)
  dds <- DESeq(dds, quiet = TRUE)
  for (ct in conc_levels[-1]) {
    res <- results(dds, contrast = c("treatment_ppm", ct, "0ppm"))
    res_dt <- as.data.table(as.data.frame(res), keep.rownames = "gene_probe_id")
    setnames(res_dt, c("baseMean", "log2FoldChange", "lfcSE", "stat", "pvalue", "padj"), c("base_mean", "log2FoldChange", "lfcSE", "stat", "pvalue", "padj"))
    res_dt[, `:=`(timepoint = tp, concentration = ct, contrast_gene_id = paste(tp, ct, gene_probe_id, sep = "|"))]
    res_dt <- res_dt[, .(contrast_gene_id, timepoint, concentration, gene_probe_id, base_mean, log2FoldChange, lfcSE, stat, pvalue, padj)]
    sig <- res_dt[!is.na(padj) & padj < 0.10]
    deseq_out_list[[length(deseq_out_list) + 1L]] <- sig
    contrast_results[[paste(tp, ct, sep = "|")]] <- res_dt
  }
}
deseq_sig <- rbindlist(deseq_out_list, use.names = TRUE, fill = TRUE)
setorder(deseq_sig, timepoint, concentration, padj, gene_probe_id)
fwrite(deseq_sig, out_de)

#-----------------------------
# Pathway analysis

# Results are generated per timepoint/concentration contrast

ora_out_list <- list()

for (key in names(contrast_results)) {
  tp <- strsplit(key, "|", fixed = TRUE)[[1]][1]
  ct <- strsplit(key, "|", fixed = TRUE)[[1]][2]
  res_dt <- contrast_results[[key]]

  # collapse to one probe per mouse gene and then one human ortholog per mouse/human gene
  human_res <- collapse_to_human(res_dt, probe_map, orth)
  if (nrow(human_res) == 0) next

  human_rank <- human_res$stat
  names(human_rank) <- human_res$human_symbol
  human_rank <- human_rank[!is.na(human_rank)]
  human_rank <- sort(human_rank, decreasing = TRUE)

  # canonical PIANO
  piano_tab <- run_piano_contrast(human_rank, canon_gsc, tp, ct)
  if (nrow(piano_tab)) piano_out_list[[length(piano_out_list) + 1L]] <- piano_tab

  # canonical ORA
  sig_all <- unique(human_res[!is.na(padj) & padj < 0.10, human_symbol])
  sig_up <- unique(human_res[!is.na(padj) & padj < 0.10 & log2FoldChange > 0, human_symbol])
  sig_down <- unique(human_res[!is.na(padj) & padj < 0.10 & log2FoldChange < 0, human_symbol])
  ora_tab <- run_ora_contrast(sig_all, sig_up, sig_down, human_background, canon_sets, tp, ct, keep_all = FALSE)
  if (nrow(ora_tab)) ora_out_list[[length(ora_out_list) + 1L]] <- ora_tab
}

piano_out <- if (length(piano_out_list)) rbindlist(piano_out_list, use.names = TRUE, fill = TRUE) else data.table()
if (nrow(piano_out)) setorder(piano_out, timepoint, concentration, direction, fdr, gene_set)
fwrite(piano_out, out_piano)

ora_out <- if (length(ora_out_list)) rbindlist(ora_out_list, use.names = TRUE, fill = TRUE) else data.table()
if (nrow(ora_out)) setorder(ora_out, timepoint, concentration, direction, fdr, gene_set)

2026-11-15T03:40:09.387058+00:00 · text_editor · { "command": "create", "path": "/workspace/outputs/METHODS.md", "file_text": "# Methods\n\nAll analyses were executed in the
{
  "command": "create",
  "path": "/workspace/outputs/METHODS.md",
  "file_text": "# Methods\n\nAll analyses were executed in the provided Linux environment with R 4.2.2 Patched and the package versions available in that environment. The workflow was written to remain compatible with the R 4.0.2 APIs requested in the benchmark, but the installed package versions were newer in several cases. The final analysis script was `work/analysis_pipeline.R`. Raw input files under `data/` were treated as read-only; any file that needed transformation was copied or derived into `work/` first. Unless otherwise noted, all statistical thresholds were Benjamini–Hochberg false discovery rate (FDR) < 0.10.\n\n## tempo_seq_filtered_counts\n\nThe filtered TempO-Seq probe-count matrix was generated from the 85 staged single-end FASTQ files in `data/raw/GSE154899/fastq/` and the sample/run metadata in `data/metadata/sample_metadata.tsv` and `data/metadata/ena_run_metadata.tsv`. The BioSpyder mouse whole-transcriptome v1.1 manifest in `data/metadata/temposeq/100770_mus_musculus_wt_11_full_manifest_revB.csv` supplied the probe reference (`PROBE_SEQUENCE`) and row key (`PROBE_NAME`).\n\nA probe FASTA was written to `work/probe_reference.fa` by extracting `PROBE_NAME` and `PROBE_SEQUENCE` from the manifest. A Bowtie 1.3.0 index was then built from that FASTA using the binary bundled with `Rbowtie` 1.38.0 (`/usr/local/lib/R/site-library/Rbowtie/bowtie-build`), yielding `work/probe_reference_index.*.ebwt`. Library-to-run mapping was assembled in `work/sample_run_map.tsv` by joining `sample_metadata.tsv` and `ena_run_metadata.tsv`, after which each staged FASTQ was processed in sample order.\n\nReads were aligned in a custom streaming pipeline using the Bowtie 1.3.0 executable bundled with `Rbowtie` 1.38.0 (`/usr/local/lib/R/site-library/Rbowtie/bowtie`) and gzipped FASTQs were decompressed with `pigz`. The alignment settings were chosen to match the QuasR TempO-Seq defaults requested by the benchmark: `-q -n 2 -l 28 -m 1 --best --strata -p 2`. Unique alignments only were retained (`-m 1`); reads with no valid unique hit were discarded. Aligned reads were counted per manifest `PROBE_NAME` and accumulated into the integer count table `work/tempo_seq_counts.dat`, with progress tracked in `work/tempo_seq_counts_progress.json`. Per-sample alignment quality summaries were written to `work/tempo_seq_bowtie_qc.tsv` and show the number of reads processed, uniquely aligned, and reported by Bowtie for each library.\n\nThe final matrix was written as `outputs/tempo_seq_filtered_counts.csv` with 30,146 probe rows and 85 sample columns. The first column is `gene_probe_id` and all remaining columns are the post-QC library IDs from `sample_metadata.tsv`. No normalization, gene collapsing, or probe filtering beyond the manifest-defined probe set was applied.\n\n## deseq2_significant_degs\n\nDifferential expression was computed from `outputs/tempo_seq_filtered_counts.csv` using DESeq2 1.38.3 in R 4.2.2. The design was fit separately within each timepoint (`day7`, `day28`, `day90`) using `DESeqDataSetFromMatrix(countData = round(counts), colData = ..., design = ~ treatment_ppm)`. The treatment factor was ordered as `0ppm`, `40ppm`, `200ppm`, `600ppm`, `2000ppm`, and `6000ppm`. For each timepoint, Wald contrasts were calculated against the matched `0ppm` control with `results(dds, contrast = c(\"treatment_ppm\", treated, \"0ppm\"))`. # Methods

All analyses were executed in the provided Linux environment with R 4.2.2 Patched and the package versions available in that environment. The workflow was written to remain compatible with the R 4.0.2 APIs requested in the benchmark, but the installed package versions were newer in several cases. The final analysis script was `work/analysis_pipeline.R`. Raw input files under `data/` were treated as read-only; any file that needed transformation was copied or derived into `work/` first. Unless otherwise noted, all statistical thresholds were Benjamini–Hochberg false discovery rate (FDR) < 0.10.

## tempo_seq_filtered_counts

The filtered TempO-Seq probe-count matrix was generated from the 85 staged single-end FASTQ files in `data/raw/GSE154899/fastq/` and the sample/run metadata in `data/metadata/sample_metadata.tsv` and `data/metadata/ena_run_metadata.tsv`. The BioSpyder mouse whole-transcriptome v1.1 manifest in `data/metadata/temposeq/100770_mus_musculus_wt_11_full_manifest_revB.csv` supplied the probe reference (`PROBE_SEQUENCE`) and row key (`PROBE_NAME`).

A probe FASTA was written to `work/probe_reference.fa` by extracting `PROBE_NAME` and `PROBE_SEQUENCE` from the manifest. A Bowtie 1.3.0 index was then built from that FASTA using the binary bundled with `Rbowtie` 1.38.0 (`/usr/local/lib/R/site-library/Rbowtie/bowtie-build`), yielding `work/probe_reference_index.*.ebwt`. Library-to-run mapping was assembled in `work/sample_run_map.tsv` by joining `sample_metadata.tsv` and `ena_run_metadata.tsv`, after which each staged FASTQ was processed in sample order.

Reads were aligned in a custom streaming pipeline using the Bowtie 1.3.0 executable bundled with `Rbowtie` 1.38.0 (`/usr/local/lib/R/site-library/Rbowtie/bowtie`) and gzipped FASTQs were decompressed with `pigz`. The alignment settings were chosen to match the QuasR TempO-Seq defaults requested by the benchmark: `-q -n 2 -l 28 -m 1 --best --strata -p 2`. Unique alignments only were retained (`-m 1`); reads with no valid unique hit were discarded. Aligned reads were counted per manifest `PROBE_NAME` and accumulated into the integer count table `work/tempo_seq_counts.dat`, with progress tracked in `work/tempo_seq_counts_progress.json`. Per-sample alignment quality summaries were written to `work/tempo_seq_bowtie_qc.tsv` and show the number of reads processed, uniquely aligned, and reported by Bowtie for each library.

The final matrix was written as `outputs/tempo_seq_filtered_counts.csv` with 30,146 probe rows and 85 sample columns. The first column is `gene_probe_id` and all remaining columns are the post-QC library IDs from `sample_metadata.tsv`. No normalization, gene collapsing, or probe filtering beyond the manifest-defined probe set was applied.

## deseq2_significant_degs

Differential expression was computed from `outputs/tempo_seq_filtered_counts.csv` using DESeq2 1.38.3 in R 4.2.2. The design was fit separately within each timepoint (`day7`, `day28`, `day90`) using `DESeqDataSetFromMatrix(countData = round(counts), colData = ..., design = ~ treatment_ppm)`. The treatment factor was ordered as `0ppm`, `40ppm`, `200ppm`, `600ppm`, `2000ppm`, and `6000ppm`. For each timepoint, Wald contrasts were calculated against the matched `0ppm` control with `results(dds, contrast = c("treatment_ppm", treated, "0ppm"))`. DESeq2 default size-factor estimation, dispersion fitting, and independent filtering were used; no LFC shrinkage was applied. The benchmark FDR criterion was implemented by retaining only rows with `padj < 0.10`.

The probe-level DESeq2 results were assembled into `outputs/deseq2_significant_degs.csv` with the requested row key `contrast_gene_id = timepoint|concentration|gene_probe_id`. Columns retained were `contrast_gene_id`, `timepoint`, `concentration`, `gene_probe_id`, `base_mean`, `log2FoldChange`, `lfcSE`, `stat`, `pvalue`, and `padj`. The file contains 2,840 significant probe-level rows across the treated-vs-control contrasts represented in the staged cohort.

## piano_preranked_gsea

Directional pre-ranked pathway enrichment was performed on the DESeq2 Wald statistics using PIANO 2.14.0. Because live Ensembl archive queries through `biomaRt` 2.54.0 were blocked by the proxy, mouse-to-human orthology was obtained with `babelgene` 22.9 as a documented fallback. The ortholog map was written to `work/mouse_to_human_ortholog_map.tsv` using `babelgene::orthologs(mouse_genes, species = "mouse", human = FALSE, min_support = 3, top = TRUE)`, retaining mouse genes with supported human orthologs. The resulting map contains `mouse_symbol`, `human_symbol`, and `support_n` and defines the human background universe in `work/canonical_background_genes.txt`.

Canonical human gene sets were obtained from the MSigDB v6.2 GMT downloads cached under `approved_web/`:

- `https://data.broadinstitute.org/gsea-msigdb/msigdb/release/6.2/h.all.v6.2.symbols.gmt`
- `https://data.broadinstitute.org/gsea-msigdb/msigdb/release/6.2/c2.cp.v6.2.symbols.gmt`

These GMTs were parsed into `work/hallmark_v6.2_gene_sets.tsv` and `work/c2cp_v6.2_gene_sets.tsv`, combined, intersected with the ortholog background, and filtered to gene sets with 5 to 500 mapped genes. The filtered collection was written to `work/canonical_gene_sets_human_filtered.tsv` and `work/canonical_gene_sets_human_filtered.gmt`; 1,370 canonical gene sets were retained.

For each timepoint/concentration contrast, probe-level DESeq2 results were first collapsed to one mouse gene symbol and then to one human ortholog by retaining the row with the largest absolute Wald statistic within each symbol. This collapsed human-ranked vector was supplied to `piano::runGSA(geneLevelStats = rank_vec, gsc = gsc, geneSetStat = "fgsea", nPerm = 1000, gsSizeLim = c(5, 500), verbose = FALSE)`. `GSAsummaryTable()` output was then transformed into directional results: `direction = up` when `Stat (dist.dir) > 0` and `direction = down` otherwise. The reported enrichment p-value and FDR were taken from the corresponding directional columns (`p (dist.dir.up)`/`p adj (dist.dir.up)` or `p (dist.dir.dn)`/`p adj (dist.dir.dn)`), and only rows with directional FDR < 0.10 were retained. `genes_up`, `genes_down`, and the corresponding percentages were taken directly from the PIANO summary table.

The final file `outputs/piano_preranked_gsea.csv` contains 867 significant directional gene-set rows with columns `gsea_result_id`, `timepoint`, `concentration`, `direction`, `gene_set`, `genes_total`, `stat_dist_dir`, `p_value`, `fdr`, `genes_up`, `genes_down`, `percent_up_genes`, and `percent_down_genes`. The standard fgseaSimple warning emitted by PIANO when `nPerm` is specified was non-fatal and did not affect the output.

## hypergeometric_pathway_enrichment

Canonical pathway overrepresentation analysis was performed on the same human-collapsed DESeq2 results and canonical gene sets used for PIANO. The analysis used the full human ortholog background in `work/canonical_background_genes.txt` and the filtered canonical gene sets in `work/canonical_gene_sets_human_filtered.tsv`/`.gmt`. For each contrast, three gene lists were constructed from the human-collapsed DESeq2 results: all significant genes (`padj < 0.10`), up-regulated significant genes (`padj < 0.10` and `log2FoldChange > 0`), and down-regulated significant genes (`padj < 0.10` and `log2FoldChange < 0`).

For each gene set, a one-sided hypergeometric overrepresentation test was computed with the background size equal to the ortholog universe, the overlap equal to the number of significant genes in the set, and the relevant contrast-specific significant gene list size. Benjamini–Hochberg correction was applied within each contrast and direction across the tested canonical gene sets. Only rows with FDR < 0.10 were retained. Direction labels in the output are `non_directional`, `up`, and `down`.

The resulting file `outputs/hypergeometric_pathway_enrichment.csv` contains 1,421 significant rows with columns `hypergeometric_result_id`, `timepoint`, `concentration`, `direction`, `gene_set`, `p_value`, `fdr`, and `significant_in_gene_set`. The row key is formatted as `timepoint|concentration|dir
2026-11-15T03:40:18.194850+00:00 · bash_session · { "action": "read" }
{
  "action": "read"
}

ection|gene_set`.

## dna_damage_enrichment

Curated DNA-damage and repair gene sets were derived from the staged workbook `data/metadata/supplementary/mmc3_dna_damage_gene_sets.xlsx` (sheet `Table S4. DNA damage gene sets`). The workbook was flattened into `work/dna_damage_gene_sets_raw.tsv` by extracting the gene-set name from column 1 and concatenating non-empty member genes from the remaining columns with semicolons. The gene set `GO_CELLULAR_RESPONSE_TO_DNA_DAMAGE_STIMULUS` was excluded per the benchmark instructions. The filtered collection was written to `work/dna_damage_gene_sets_human_filtered.tsv`; 88 gene sets remained. The DNA-damage background universe was defined exactly as the union of all genes present in these 88 retained gene sets and written to `work/dna_damage_background_genes.txt` (1,292 genes).

The same hypergeometric procedure described above was then applied to the 13 required contrast/timepoint combinations: day7 600ppm, day7 2000ppm, day7 6000ppm; all nonzero day28 concentrations (40, 200, 600, 2000, and 6000 ppm); and all nonzero day90 concentrations (40, 200, 600, 2000, and 6000 ppm). For each contrast and gene set, three tests were reported separately: non-directional, up-regulated, and down-regulated. Benjamini–Hochberg FDR was computed within each contrast and direction across the 88 curated gene sets, and no significance filter was applied at the output stage so that all requested test combinations were retained.

The final file `outputs/dna_damage_enrichment.csv` contains 1,144 rows, corresponding to 13 contrasts × 88 retained gene sets. Required columns are `dna_damage_result_id`, `timepoint`, `concentration`, `gene_set`, `p_value_non_directional`, `fdr_non_directional`, `significant_in_gene_set_non_directional`, `p_value_up`, `fdr_up`, `significant_in_gene_set_up`, `p_value_down`, `fdr_down`, and `significant_in_gene_set_down`.
